=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an array of integers nums.
Your task is to find the length of the longest subsequence seq of nums, such that the absolute differences between consecutive elements form a non-increasing sequence of integers. In other words, for a subsequence seq_0, seq_1, seq_2, ..., seq_m of nums, |seq_1 - seq_0| >= |seq_2 - seq_1| >= ... >= |seq_m - seq_m - 1|.
Return the length of such a subsequence.
A subsequence is an non-empty array that can be derived from another array by deleting some or no elements without changing the order of the remaining elements.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [16,6,3]
Output: 3
Explanation: 
The longest subsequence is [16, 6, 3] with the absolute adjacent differences [10, 3].

Example 2:

Input: nums = [6,5,3,4,2,1]
Output: 4
Explanation:
The longest subsequence is [6, 4, 2, 1] with the absolute adjacent differences [2, 2, 1].

Example 3:

Input: nums = [10,20,10,19,10,20]
Output: 5
Explanation: 
The longest subsequence is [10, 20, 10, 19, 10] with the absolute adjacent differences [10, 10, 9, 9].

 
Constraints:

2 <= nums.length <= 10^4
1 <= nums[i] <= 300

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def longestSubsequence(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestSubsequence(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        MAXV = 300
        MAXD = 300
        dp = [[0] * MAXD for _ in range(MAXV + 1)]
        suffix = [[0] * MAXD for _ in range(MAXV + 1)]
        seen = [False] * (MAXV + 1)
        ans = 1

        for x in nums:
            temp = [0] * MAXD
            # Query previous values
            for y in range(1, MAXV + 1):
                if not seen[y]:
                    continue
                d = abs(x - y)
                best = suffix[y][d]
                # best >= 1
                cand = best + 1
                if cand > temp[d]:
                    temp[d] = cand
                    if cand > ans:
                        ans = cand

            # Apply updates
            for d in range(MAXD):
                if temp[d] > dp[x][d]:
                    dp[x][d] = temp[d]

            seen[x] = True

            # Recompute suffix for value x
            run = 0
            # local variables for speed
            dp_x = dp[x]
            suf_x = suffix[x]
            for d in range(MAXD - 1, -1, -1):
                if dp_x[d] > run:
                    run = dp_x[d]
                # length-1 subsequence ending at x is always possible
                if run < 1:
                    suf_x[d] = 1
                else:
                    suf_x[d] = run

        return ans

Let's define DP invariant:
After processing elements up to index i:
- seen[v] true iff v appears in nums[0..i].
- dp[v][d] = maximum length of a valid subsequence ending at some processed element with value v, and whose last absolute difference is exactly d. If no such subsequence, 0.
- suffix[v][d] = max(1 if seen[v] else 0, max_{e>=d} dp[v][e]).
Actually suffix[v][d] = maximum length of a valid subsequence ending at value v that can be followed by a next difference of d, i.e., either length1 (no previous diff) or last diff >= d. This is exactly what we need.

When processing new x:
For each previous value y, let d=|x-y|. Any valid subsequence ending at y that can be followed by diff d has length suffix[y][d]. Appending x gives length suffix[y][d]+1 with new exact diff d. We take max over y for each d. This computes best new subsequences ending at x with exact diff d. We update dp[x][d]. Then recompute suffix[x]. This maintains invariant.

Need ensure suffix[v][d] = max(1 if seen[v] else 0, max_{e>=d} dp[v][e]) is correct for "can be followed by next difference d". For a subsequence of length 1, no previous diff, so can be followed by any d. For length >=2, condition is last diff e >= d. So yes.

Now, let's think about if dp[v][d] for length2 with exact diff d can be followed by next diff <=d. suffix handles. Good.

Before final, let's maybe try to prove with a tricky case where suffixMax includes length1 and dp exact diff from same value but different occurrences. Example nums [1,2,1,3]. Let's brute:
Valid subsequences:
[1,2,1,3]: diffs1,1,2 -> 1>=1 but 1>=2 false invalid.
[1,2,3]: diffs1,1 valid length3.
[1,1,3]: diffs0,2 invalid.
[2,1,3]: diffs1,2 invalid.
[1,2,1]: diffs1,1 valid length3.
[1,2,3] length3. Algorithm:
1 seen.
2: y1 d1 L2 dp2[1]=2.
1: y1 d0 suffix1[0]=1 L2; y2 d1 suffix2[1]=2 L3 dp1[1]=3.
3: y1 d2 suffix1[2]=1? dp1[1]=3, suffix1[2]=1 (exact1<2) L2; y2 d1 suffix2[1]=2 L3; y3? unseen. ans3. Good. It doesn't produce invalid [1,2,1,3] because suffix1[2]=1 not 3. Good.

Now, consider [1,3,2,4]. Valid [1,3,2,4] diffs2,1,2 invalid; [1,3,4] diffs2,1 valid length3; [1,2,4] diffs1,2 invalid; [3,2,4] diffs1,2 invalid; [1,3,2] length3. Algorithm:
1 seen.
3: y1 d2 L2 dp3[2]=2.
2: y1 d1 suffix1[1]=1 L2; y3 d1 suffix3[1]=2 L3 dp2[1]=3.
4: y1 d3 suffix1[3]=1 L2; y3 d1 suffix3[1]=2 L3; y2 d2 suffix2[2]=1? dp2[1]=3, suffix2[2]=1 (exact1<2) L2. ans3. Good.

Now, consider [1,4,2,3]. Valid [1,4,2,3] diffs3,2,1 length4. Algorithm:
1 seen.
4: y1 d3 L2 dp4[3]=2.

3: y1 d2 suffix1[2]=1 L2; y4 d1 suffix4[1]=2 L3; y2 d1 suffix2[1]=3 (exact2>=1) L4. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "suffix" for x after recompute if seen[x] True but dp[x] has exact diff d values from subsequences ending at current x and earlier x. For future, both are before future. Good
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
with the given examples:

Example 1: [16,6,3] -> 3
Example 2: [6,5,3,4,2,1] -> 4
Example 3: [10,20,10,19,10,20] -> 5

The DP approach should handle all cases correctly.

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestSubsequence(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        MAXV = 300
        MAXD = 300

        # dp[v][d] = max length of a valid subsequence ending with value v
        # and whose last absolute difference is exactly d.
        dp = [[0] * MAXD for _ in range(MAXV + 1)]

        # suffix[v][d] = max length of a valid subsequence ending with value v
        # that can be followed by a difference of d, i.e. last diff >= d.
        # Also includes length-1 subsequences for seen values.
        suffix = [[0] * MAXD for _ in range(MAXV + 1)]

        seen = [False] * (MAXV + 1)
        ans = 1

        for x in nums:
            temp = [0] * MAXD

            # Try appending x to every possible previous value y.
            for y in range(1, MAXV + 1):
                d = abs(x - y)
                best = suffix[y][d]
                if best:
                    cand = best + 1
                    if cand > temp[d]:
                        temp[d] = cand
                    if cand > ans:
                        ans = cand

            # Update exact-difference DP for current value x.
            for d in range(MAXD):
                if temp[d] > dp[x][d]:
                    dp[x][d] = temp[d]

            # Mark x as seen: a length-1 subsequence ending at x exists.
            seen[x] = True

            # Recompute suffix for value x.
            run = 0
            dp_x = dp[x]
            suf_x = suffix[x]
            for d in range(MAXD - 1, -1, -1):
                if dp_x[d] > run:
                    run = dp_x[d]
                # length-1 subsequence is always usable for any future diff
                suf_x[d] = run if run > 1 else 1

        return ans
```
